# Proposed OEIS comments: A183715 to A188689

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A183715

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183715>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [6077](../papers/06001-06500/06077-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n)=2*a(n-1)+54*a(n-2)-2*a(n-3)-738*a(n-4)-312*a(n-5)+4078*a(n-6)+2098*a(n-7)-10802*a(n-8)-4874*a(n-9)+14854*a(n-10)+4874*a(n-11)-10802*a(n-12)-2098*a(n-13)+4078*a(n-14)+312*a(n-15)-738*a(n-16)+2*a(n-17)+54*a(n-18)-2*a(n-19)-a(n-20).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 15 626 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 20. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [9225](../papers/09001-09500/09225-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - 22*a(n-3) + 18*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(31 - 26*x - 134*x^2 + 140*x^3) / (1 - 5*x - x^2 + 22*x^3 - 18*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Apr 05 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is a condition on every subblock of a fixed size, so a bounded window of consecutive lines decides it, and counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [2162](../papers/02001-02500/02162-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) - 60*a(n-3) + 85*a(n-4) + 150*a(n-5) - 360*a(n-6) + 396*a(n-8) - 216*a(n-9).
> Empirical g.f.: 3*x*(57 - 239*x - 425*x^2 + 2673*x^3 - 234*x^4 - 8968*x^5 + 5676*x^6 + 9252*x^7 - 7992*x^8) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 - 2*x^2)*(1 - 3*x^2)*(1 - 6*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Apr 09 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A184018

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A184018>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11737](../papers/11501-12000/11737-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) + 2*(1-3*n)*a(n-1) + 3*(n-1)*a(n-2) + 2*(3*n-5)*a(n-3) + (n-3)*a(n-4) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 15 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 14. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A184120

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A184120>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10398](../papers/10001-10500/10398-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) +2*(2n+1)*a(n-1) +4*(n-1)*a(n-2) +4*(2n-5)*a(n-3) +4*(n-3)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 17 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A184130

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A184130>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2215](../papers/02001-02500/02215-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) - 36*a(n-2) + 84*a(n-3) - 126*a(n-4) + 126*a(n-5) - 84*a(n-6) + 36*a(n-7) - 9*a(n-8) + a(n-9) (=polynomial of degree 8).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Apr 12 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 18 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A184189

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A184189>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9458](../papers/09001-09500/09458-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2) - 2*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 + x)*(3 - 4*x) / (1 - 4*x + 2*x^2 + 2*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Apr 12 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 3 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A185089

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A185089>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11659](../papers/11501-12000/11659-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> conjecture: (n+2)*a(n) -3*(n+1)*a(n-1) +3*(2-n)*a(n-2) +(11*n-20)*a(n-3) +(11-5*n)*a(n-4) +(4-n)*a(n-5) =0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 16 2011 (Formula verified and used for computations. - _Fung Lam_, Feb 24 2014)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A185369

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A185369>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9599](../papers/09501-10000/09599-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +2*a(n) +4*(-n+1)*a(n-1) +2*(n-1)*(n-3)*a(n-2) +(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A186341

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A186341>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11554](../papers/11501-12000/11554-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+2)*a(n) +5*(-n-1)*a(n-1) +2*(-n+3)*a(n-2) +(38*n-59)*a(n-3) +(-22*n+41)*a(n-4) +4*(-22*n+81)*a(n-5) +3*(19*n-79)*a(n-6) +3*(29*n-164)*a(n-7) +2*(-17*n+98)*a(n-8) +16*(-2*n+15)*a(n-9)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 08 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 7, so the recurrence holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A187252

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A187252>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9606](../papers/09501-10000/09606-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence a(n) +(-2*n-3)*a(n-1) +(n+7)*(n-1)*a(n-2) +4*(-n^2+2*n+1)*a(n-3) +4*(n-3)^2*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 22 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A188312

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A188312>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11738](../papers/11501-12000/11738-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) +(3-4*n)*a(n-1) + (7-6*n)*a(n-2) -a(n-3) +(n-4)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 15 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 0, so the recurrence holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
